> **For coding agents and contributors:** This is the **only** UI design doc. If you're adding a new page or amending an existing one, this file dictates the colors, fonts, components, and page anatomy. Do not invent parallel design notes — extend this doc instead. See `## Implementation rules — read this first` below.

---

## Implementation rules — read this first

### No descriptions on the page (v3.75, 2026-10-12) — overrides every subtitle, lede and hint described below

The user, circling six of them on HR's pages: "find all this wording … like a
description, i want delete it, cause this cause the view too messy". A page
says what it is with its eyebrow and title, a section with its heading, a tab
with its label, a menu row with its words — never a sentence under them that
explains what the page or section is, how a tab works, what a rule means, why
the screen looks as it does, or where to click. Gone from every portal page
and every view (the phone apps — Revenue app, HR app — were not part of it):

- **Page subtitles** — `PageHead subtitle`, `.page-sub`, a sentence under the
  `page-title`. A subtitle stays only when it is live data (*Signed in as …*,
  a voucher's own description, *{borrower} · {bank}*).
- **Section, panel and tab ledes** — the line under a section heading or a tab
  rail (`.subtitle`, `.sheet-subtitle`, report cards' one-line descriptions).
- **Explanatory notices** — a box or a paragraph that explains a rule, a
  setting's effect or a method (*Below 30% of the order total, so none of the
  commission is released yet…*). A notice that was the only place a CURRENT
  STATE showed becomes a 2–4 word chip beside what it concerns (*Payout off
  this month* beside a tab's heading when its payout is switched off); a state
  shown elsewhere on the page (a status bar, a chip, a disabled button) loses
  the sentence outright.
- **Usage hints** — *Right-click a row to remove it.*, *Shift + right-click
  opens the browser's own menu.*, *Double-click to open · right-click for
  more*, *· highlights only*. The behaviour stays; only the words went.
- **A menu row's second line** — one line a row (the bill menu's rule of
  2026-10-12, now every menu: `RowMenu`, `row-links`, the payroll row menu);
  what it said — why a row is shut, who holds a bill, what a row does — is the
  row's `title=` tooltip. A disabled row must still take the hover for that
  tooltip to show.

What stays: errors, and warnings that name a problem to fix (with its link or
list); empty states; figures, dates, counts, chips, statuses and a stat card's
short note; a row's own note on why it differs (*Moved from ADRIAN*, *Not open
yet — …*); and everything inside a window, drawer, panel or form a click opens
— those sentences say what a write will do. PDFs, CSVs and print are documents,
not the view. A new page, or a prototype that brings a description with it,
leaves the description out (`src/lib/no-page-descriptions.test.ts`).

### Sheet theme — the portal's new tone, HR's view first (v3.9–v3.10, 2026-09-27)

Comm Cal's month (#151 → #162) is the reference for how the portal now looks,
and the user asked for every page to follow it "so that the software is all
sync with the tone" — starting with the pages HR reads: **HR Costing** (Overview,
Payroll, Payment, Allocation, payslips, import), **Director Salary**, **Director
Pool**, **Revenue** (Comm Cal and every page under a month, Staff Hierarchy,
Rules — not the Product List, which keeps its own workspace) and
**Claims** (v3.9), then the rest of HR's sidebar (v3.10): the **Overview**
(`/dashboard` — one route, so the Principal's, Finance's and a department
manager's dashboards moved with HR's) and **People** (People Management and
every person / account page and form under it, Member Services, Hierarchy,
Roles, Permissions). `lib/sheet-routes.ts` lists the prefixes; extending the
tone to the rest of the portal is adding a prefix there and converting the
page (below).

**v3.12 (2026-10-07) — Finance's view, by prefix only.** The user asked for
Finance's background to match the Principal's and HR's ("做 A 先"), so every
remaining portal route moved onto the sheet: Approvals, Payments, Receipt,
Invoice / Statements, Service Billing, Billing Allocator, Invoice Pool, Daily
Bank, Fund IN / OUT, Bank BA, Overdue, Repayment Schedule, Dept Pool, Venture
Pool, OPEX, My Billing, Reports, Reconciliation, Journal Entries, Audit Log,
Settings. The sheet, its lettering, buttons and tables apply through the
overrides; those pages' anatomy is not yet rebuilt — a walnut hero or a figure
strip stays until the page is converted per the steps below, one page at a
time as the user picks them. **Converted so far:** Payments and Receipts
(`VoucherLedger`, 2026-10-07 — four summary cards, counted tabs, one toolbar,
a section head over the table); Approvals (2026-10-07 — four cards, the
pending queue and the recent decisions as `SortableTable`s with one decision
bar under the queue for what is ticked; on a phone a row is Description +
Amount with the number and who / when as small print, and the arrow opens
Type / Pool / Category / Payee / Pay by / Attachments (thumbnails) / Submitted
and the voucher); Service Billing = Invoice (`BillingView`, 2026-10-07 — the
month beside the title, every control in a captioned action band, four cards,
the payroll basis folded above the invoices with its figures on the fold, the
invoices as a tabbed `SortableTable` with a decision bar) and the monthly
Statements (four cards, a section per month); the Billing Allocator
(2026-10-07 — the month beside the title, four cards Allocated / To bill /
Billed / HV own share, a counted rail Calculate · Records · Staff · Setup,
the records as a `SortableTable` with the split as one column per company —
the user's pick over chips — HV's own share the last column in grey and the
batch as a chip, a By company table with each target's share / billed / to
bill / invoices, Calculate's preview as a table). On a phone the `.fin-queue`
pages set the opened row's labels in the card-label face and the row's one
action (Settle) as an orange pill; Finance's and the Principal's tab bar reads
in English. The user compared a Harbour-blue accent for
Finance's sheet with the orange and kept the orange ("我发现橘色比较好看"): the
sheet has one accent for every department.

**The Finance view (2026-10-09).** The user's Claude Design handoff
(`.design/finance-revamp`) redesigns every Finance page in this same language
and adds global rules that apply to **Finance's pages only** — the routes in
`lib/finance-view.ts` as Finance or the Principal sees them; HR's and a
department manager's pages keep the sheet as described above. PortalFrame
puts `.fin-view` on the sheet frame and `FinViewProvider` around the shell;
everything the view changes hangs off those (globals.css › "THE FINANCE
VIEW"). On it: a card's figure is in the number face and coloured by meaning
(ledger green for money in, danger red for money out, the new blue `wait`
for anything waiting on someone, walnut for the page's one figure), its label
sentence-case; chips in the body face, pending in blue; tables wear a navy
band (#E2E7EF / #1D2B4A) with white rows, no zebra, and offer a Range mode on
amount and date columns (`range`), a 620px scroll box with a sticky header
(`scrollBox`) and neighbour resizing (`resizeMode`); the row menu reads Open /
Open in new tab; a list keeps its scroll on Back; on a phone the top bar is
cream with the H tile and the page's name, the lit tab sits on a peach pill,
and a detail page goes back on an edge swipe. Numbers never come from the
prototype.
Its page-head menus (`ActMenu`: "From payroll ▾", "Export ▾", "+ Create
Invoice ▾") hold the controls a captioned band used to lay out; a page's
facts sit in `DetailGroups` (banded group cards of label / value rows) and
its actions in a `.fin-actions` panel with a line on what they do. Where the
handoff and an earlier per-page pick disagreed (the allocator's split: the
prototype's one text column against the 每家一栏 matrix of two days before),
the user chose the handoff — a row's breakdown is one line of "label pct%"
and a page of its own (`/billing-allocator/[id]`), not a column per company.

**What it is.** PortalFrame draws, for those routes, the content column inset
10px around ONE white sheet (`.sheet-frame` › `.sheet`, 16px radius, hairline
border, 24px padding) that stays put while one of these pages replaces another;
the loading skeleton lands on it too. The shell — sidebar rail, greeting bar and
the plaster around the sheet — keeps the Walnut/Harbour palette and follows the
user's theme (#152: the user wanted the shell's colours preserved). Inside the
sheet `.sheet-theme` declares the Comm Cal month's tokens:

- **Lettering is black** (`--ink`, `--num-ink`, `--graphite`, `--muted` are all
  0 0 0). Hierarchy comes from size, weight and surfaces. No italics — `em` and
  `.italic` stand upright on the sheet.
- **Orange `#E99A53` is a surface, never lettering** (2.2:1 on white). It paints
  primary buttons, the active tab's 3px underline over a peach wash
  (`--accent-bg` `#FFF4E7`), the page-head rule, the eyebrow numeral, checkbox
  ticks. `text-accent` renders black (links underline), `hover:text-accent`
  underlines, and anything painted orange carries black lettering even where the
  markup still says `text-white`.
- **Walnut is the one dark object**: the page's primary summary card
  (`tone="primary"`), a salesperson's / statement's person header, dark
  popovers (`bg-ink` renders walnut on the sheet).
- Semantics keep their meaning and their light values in both themes: red
  negatives and errors, green done / increases, amber waiting. A status is a
  **word on a wash** (`.chip alert|ledger|danger|info|accent` with its dot),
  never coloured words alone.
- The sheet is light in both themes, like the Product List: black lettering
  needs a light ground. Components with their own palettes (`.org-canvas`,
  `.pay-board`, `.hero-walnut`) stay at their light values on it.

**How a page is built on it** (reference: `hr-costing/page.tsx`,
`hr-costing/payroll/[period]/page.tsx`, and the month itself):

1. `.page-head` as everywhere (eyebrow `<em>I.</em> SECTION · PAGE`, Georgia
   title); on the sheet the rule is orange and the eyebrow 10px.
2. **Summary cards** (`components/sheet/StatCard.tsx`) instead of a hero, a
   figure strip or tiles: `<StatCards label>` of `<StatCard label value icon
   note tone href?>` — a short bar, the label in small caps, the figure, a note
   and a line icon (`SheetIcon`) at the foot. Exactly one `primary` per row, the
   figure the page is opened for; `danger` / `alert` only while the problem
   holds. A month shown as a hero moves into the eyebrow/title; its status line
   becomes the primary card's note.
3. Sections: `.sect-head` (17px/600 title over the same rule and mono count), or
   `.sheet-heading`; a `.sheet-subtitle` under it only for a line of live
   status or data, never a description (v3.75).
4. Tables: every `.ledger-table` becomes the month's table automatically — a
   framed well (`216 210 200`, 10px radius), an uppercase 10px/600 header band
   (`235 231 224` over a `207 197 183` rule), white / `#F7F7F5` zebra, peach
   hover, 12px text with 12px `.num` figures, and a peach `<tfoot>` for the
   total. Put a closing total in `<tfoot>`. The payroll grid keeps its own
   geometry and takes only the lettering and the light department bands
   (`.group-btn` is the header band's tone with a walnut marker on the sheet).
   `.table-frame` › `.table-frame-scroll` + `.table-frame-foot` when a table
   needs the month's footer strip. Rows grouped under a label that does not
   fold (Roles' departments) sit under a band row —
   `<tr className="band-row"><td colSpan={n}>Finance <span className="count">4 titles</span></td></tr>`
   — the header band's tone with a walnut marker, the roster's `.group-btn`
   band without the button.
5. Tabs: `SlidingTabs` renders the month's tabs on the sheet by itself (a
   tinted, underlined pick; counts in boxed badges). A hand-built rail uses
   `.sheet-tabs` with `aria-selected` / `aria-current` and `.sheet-count`.
6. `.sheet-toolbar` for a search / filter panel above a table,
   `.sheet-notice` (peach) / `.sheet-warning` (amber) / `.sheet-danger` (red)
   for a callout. A salesperson's payslip or statement opens on the walnut
   person header, as the workbench's person view does (`.hero-walnut`, 12px on
   the sheet): eyebrow row with a status tag, initials, name, title · rank,
   the total on the right.
7. Overlays portalled to `<body>` (PaycheckWindow, the commission editor, the
   hierarchy's Move popover and context menu) carry `sheet-theme` on their
   portal root, because they render outside the sheet.

Every sheet override in `globals.css` is written `:where(.sheet-theme) <base
selector>`, so it has exactly the specificity of the rule it replaces: a page's
own utilities keep the power they had. Don't add a sheet rule with more weight
than its base.

### The HR revamp — HR's view and its kit (v3.19, 2026-10-10)

The Claude Design handoff *Sidebar design revamp from HR* (`.design/hr-revamp/
handoff`: README = spec, `design/Revenue App.dc.html` = source of truth, variant
A) redraws HR's view and the pages it owns. **When the README and the file
disagree, the file wins; every value is the prototype's.**

- **HR's shell** (`lib/hr-view.ts` — the hr role, and the Principal viewing as
  HR; every other view keeps its shell). `.hrv-shell` on a 243 ground: a square,
  full-height, flush sidebar (`HrSidebar`, 248px ↔ 64px content-box on `248 245
  240` with a `229 222 211` rule) of icon tiles in five toned sections —
  Overview · I. HR & PAYROLL (clay) · II. DIRECTORS (gold) · III. REVENUE
  (slate) · IV. PEOPLE (olive) · V. ADMIN (walnut) (`components/layout/
  hr-nav.ts`). One peach pill with a 3px orange bar sits behind the rows and
  slides to the row clicked (`top .34s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)`, measured from
  the row's offsetTop); the open row's tile swaps light/deep and grows to 1.06,
  its label nudges 2px (.25s). The walnut **H** folds the rail (`width .28s`;
  the words fade `.2s` where they stand; a cookie, `vp-hr-nav`, so it draws
  folded). The brand block (H tile, HOUZS Venture, Internal ledger) has no rule
  under it and pads `20px 12px 26px 14px` — round 2's air between it and
  Overview (v3.41). The greeting bar is white (`HrTopBar`: 60px, 16px radius, 10px in);
  for an account that may switch views the "HR view" words ARE the switcher.
  The sheet is the `<main>` and scrolls inside a 10px frame (`.hrv-frame`,
  `data-hrv-overlay`) — so its rounded foot is always in view and drawers,
  windows and the toast cover the sheet only.
- **The kit** (`components/hrv/`: `kit.tsx` static pieces, `tabs.tsx`,
  `overlay.tsx`, `toast.tsx`, `icons.tsx`, one `hrv.module.css`; colours are
  the `--hv-*` tokens on `.sheet-theme`): the page head (10px/600/.18em eyebrow
  behind a 2×10 orange bar — no roman numeral — Georgia 36/1.1, a 13px subtitle
  only when it is live data (*Signed in as …*; v3.75), the 64px orange segment; `bare` when tabs sit under it), tabs (8px tops,
  peach + `inset 0 -2px 0` orange, 600; boxed counts), summary cards (the
  prototype's own sizes per page), chips (mono 11px word + 5px dot on a wash),
  grid tables (the prototype's tables are CSS grids: a `216 210 200` frame,
  10px radius, a `235 231 224` band of 10.5px/600/.04em caps over `207 197
  183`), right drawers (400–600px on `rgb(43 31 23 / .18)`), centred windows
  (`.28` / `.22`), the toast (walnut, orange ●, bottom-centre, 2.2 s —
  `flash()` after a save), the 28×16 toggle (`left .15s`), the peach save bar,
  history dots, label/value facts. Esc closes the overlay on top. On a phone
  (≤767px) the frame runs on under the 54px tab bar, so a drawer and its scrim
  end where the sheet ends (`54px + 10px + var(--safe-bottom)`), a window
  centres in the room above the bar (at most `100% − 94px − safe`) and the
  toast stands `54px + 30px + safe` up (2026-10-11).

### The HR revamp, round 2 — the kit's additions (v3.30, 2026-10-11)

The handoff's second package (`.design/hr-r2/handoff`, README sections 10–18;
the file wins over the README) adds Documentation, Hiring, Meeting Rooms, the
Guest link and an HR phone app. Two things every page of it shares:

- **The soft dropdown** (`components/hrv/select.tsx` `Select`, its own
  `select.module.css`; README 18.1, the handoff's `dropdown.js`). The real
  `<select>` stays — value, onChange, keyboard — and is drawn radius 10 (12 on
  the guest page), a 14px chevron (stroke 2.4, `#5a4e44`) 11px from the right,
  34px kept clear for it, hover border `214 202 188`, focus the accent border
  and a `0 0 0 3px rgb(233 154 83 / .18)` ring. A pointer press opens the menu
  instead of the browser's: fixed to the window, white, radius 14, a `236 229
  219` edge, `0 14px 36px rgba(43,31,23,.18), 0 2px 6px rgba(43,31,23,.06)`,
  6px padding; rows 9px 12px, radius 9, 13.5px / 1.3, hover `247 244 239`, the
  picked one peach with `163 78 37` 600 and a ✓, the blank option grey `150 138
  126`; as wide as the field, wider only when an option would be cut; a search
  box (34px of content, 36 with its edge — an input's own box, as dropdown.js;
  radius 9; the browser's black; its own orange ring on focus) over more than
  eight options; below the field, 6px away — above it when the whole menu does
  not fit below and there is more room above, its list sized to that side
  (v3.40: dropdown.js opened below whenever 200px were free, and on a phone a
  long list ran off the screen); `hvddIn .16s` (fade + 4px drop). The menu
  carries `data-soft-menu`, so the portal's own field rules (the blue focus
  glow, the phone's 44px floor) leave it as drawn, as they leave the Revenue
  app, the HR app and the guest page (`data-guest-page`). It closes
  on a pick, a press outside, Esc (the menu only — a drawer under it stays), a
  resize or a scroll. Arrow keys and Enter work inside it. **On a touch screen**
  (`pointer: coarse`, v3.58, the user — the Revenue app's book sheet would not
  open Department or From / To): the search box waits for a tap instead of
  taking the focus on opening (that raised the keyboard, whose resize closed
  the menu at once — every list over eight), and a resize or a scroll moves
  the menu with its field, inside what the keyboard leaves showing (the visual
  viewport), closing only once the field has left the screen.
  **Every select on HR's pages is one since v3.42** (round 2's Part 1,
  2026-10-11): round 1's pages, HR Costing, the month's workbench, People,
  Member services, Audit Log and the top bar's view switch —
  `soft-select-coverage.test.ts` parses them and fails on a bare `<select>`.
  `bare` keeps the page's own field box (the soft field's radius, chevron and
  ring over it); `plain` keeps the field exactly as drawn and swaps only the
  menu — for selects the prototype draws as something else (the Members
  grid's 6px boxes with a ▾, Scheme rules' quiet values, Brand weeks' "Revenue
  · Fair" chip, the top bar's "HR view" words). An `<optgroup>`'s label heads
  its options in the menu (10.5px / 600 / .08em caps `117 100 86`, 8px 12px
  4px — dropdown.js flattened them; People's Invite and Audit Log's Record pick
  by group). The focus ring is `!important` too, so the portal's .12 glow does
  not cover the prototype's .18. A popover that closes on a press or a scroll
  outside itself asks `inSoftMenu(target)` first — the menu lives on `<body>`
  (the bill menu's Share list).
- **Motion** (README 18.2): every right drawer slides in 40px and fades
  (`hvDrawerIn .34s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)`), its scrim fades in `.25s`, a
  centred window fades in `.2s` — the kit's `Drawer` / `Window` do it, so
  round 1's drawers move the same way. Reduced motion turns them off.

### Documentation (v3.31, 2026-10-11)

People › Documentation, `/team/documentation` (README §10; the handoff's
`Revenue App.dc.html` lines 703–900, the preview 1498–1552). The kit head
(eyebrow *People · Documentation*), compact tabs Documents · Data center ·
Letters · Publish with no counts (`margin: -4px 0 18px`). Values in
`team/documentation/_components/documentation.module.css`; cards, the table and
the quiet buttons have NO fill of their own — they sit on the white sheet.

- **Documents**: `1fr 1.5fr`, gap 20. Cards: 1px hairline, radius 12, 18px
  20px, a 15px / 500 title. Upload a document: Name (8px 10px, radius 6), a
  dashed file box (*No file chosen* · *PDF, Excel, Word or image*, Upload →
  Replace in a white 7px 14px button), department chips (6px 12px, radius 7,
  gap 6 — the ☑/☐ its own item; peach with an orange rim when on), Save (the
  accent, 8px 18px) — the 12px note under it went in v3.75. The list: a row per file — a 38px olive
  tile (`238 240 226` / `88 98 68`, mono 10px 500 extension), the name 500, a

### Hiring › Requests (v3.32, 2026-10-11)

Hiring › Requests, `/hiring/requests` (README §11; the handoff's `Revenue
App.dc.html` lines 980–1144, the windows 1365–1497), HR's sidebar section
**V. HIRING** in clay. Values in `hiring/_components/hiring.module.css`; the
prototype's inputs are content-box, so ours are too (a width is the text's).
The page head is *HIRING · REQUESTS* / *Hiring requests* / *Manpower requests
from every department, from submission to filled.*; three compact tabs
(`?tab=`): **Requests · Listing · Form settings** (the last only for Hiring ·
Edit).

- **Requests** — the review inbox, `340px | 1fr`, gap 20. The left card
  (radius 12, 14px): *Incoming* 15px 500 and *+ New request* (7px 12px,
  12.5px, hairline); the filters as pills (5px 11px, 12px; the picked one
  peach with an accent edge) with a mono 11px count — Pending · Info
  requested · Declined; rows radius 9, 10px 12px, the title 500 cut with an
  ellipsis, the status chip (mono 10.5px), `Revenue · IR · Kar Jun · 9 Oct
  2026` 11.5px brown; the picked row peach. The right card (20px 22px): the id
  mono 11px, the position 20px 500, *From Kar Jun · Revenue · submitted 9 Oct
  2026* 13px, the chip; tiles `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(160px, 1fr))` in 1px
  hairline seams, radius 8 — Budget, Headcount and Expected start first in
  peach with clay label and value 16px, then Company, Department and the rest
  (11.5px brown label, 600 13.5px value in `51 36 27`); the long answers under
  10.5px caps labels (14px / 1.65, 72ch); an attachment row (a 34px olive FILE
  tile, Download). Under a hairline: **Owner** (`240px`; HR's own people) ·
  **Note to requester**, then **Acknowledge** (accent, 8px 16px), **Request
  more info**, **Decline** (danger edge) and, at the right, *Open full page*
  underlined; the refusal in red 12.5px. *History*: one line a step — the date
  mono 11px in 80px, the step (600 when it is a status), who, the note.
- **Listing** — five stat cards (Total, Pending in alert, Acknowledged,
  Filled in ledger, Cancelled in danger; 24px 500; a press filters, the
  picked one peach). The table radius 10 in a `216 210 200` edge: columns
  `80px 1.5fr 1fr×6 130px`, a `235 231 224` head (10.5px 600 caps .04em);
  rows 13px, hover `250 248 245`, a cancelled one at .6; the id mono 11.5px,
  the position 500 with *Hired: …* or the employment type under it, the owner
  (*Unassigned* in alert 500), the budget mono 12px, candidates `2 · 1 appt ·
  1 out`, the chip. A press opens the request's page; a right-press opens the
  menu (white, radius 8, 4px, at 70% / 240px): Acknowledge, Mark filled…,
  Cancel request (danger), Reopen, Open details (`90 78 68`).
- **Form settings** — a card at most 980px: *What requesters fill in* +
  *Preview form*; the columns `56px 1.4fr 140px 1.6fr 110px 34px` (↑↓,
  the field's name, its answer type — Short text, Number, Long text, Choice,
  Range (from – to) — the choices or a dash, a Required switch 28×16 `.2s`,
  ×); Position and Department say *Core* instead of ×. *+ Add field* under
  it. A second card, **Hiring sources**: cards `minmax(220px, 1fr)` with the
  name, `2 hires` mono 11px, an On / Off switch and × (only while unused).
  Every change saves itself.
- **+ New request** — the 640px drawer (head 18px 22px, *New hiring request*
  19px): *Request manpower* · *Fields come from Form settings*; Requested by
  (`Grace Lim · HR`, at most 280px); Form settings' fields two to a row, a
  long answer across both, a range as From *to* To (90px each), required ones
  starred in red; **Submit to HR** (8px 18px) with the refusal beside it.
- **A request's page** (`/hiring/requests/HR-203`): *‹ All requests*, the id
  mono 11.5px, the title 24px (`Revenue Specialist × 2`), *Requested by …*,
  an owner pill (peach / clay, or *No owner yet* in alert) and the chip; then
  `1fr | 340px` (the right column sticky). Left: **Requirements** (tiles
  `minmax(170px, 1fr)`, the three highlighted 17px, values 14px; a filled one
  adds Hired through and Hiring cost), the long answers as their own cards
  (14px / 1.7), **Candidates · N** with **+** (an accent 30px square: *＋ New
  candidate*, *⇣ Pick from Talent Bank*) and **⋯** (Candidate fields). A
  candidate is a card (radius 10, a 3px left edge in their stage's colour):
  38px initials in the stage's tones, the name 600 and a stage chip (11px
  500), `Applied · 016-221 0457`, their details as pills (`246 244 241`,
  *Expected salary RM 3,000*), the note behind a 2px accent rule; at the
  right *View résumé* and **⋯** — Edit details, Change status ▸ (the five
  stages, the current one 600), Download résumé, Move to Talent Bank… / Open
  in Talent Bank. Both menus hang 6px under their button, and open 6px ABOVE it
  when the sheet shows no room below (v3.75 — at the foot of a request the +
  ran off the page, and an open menu's catcher keeps the wheel from scrolling
  the sheet; `menu-place.ts`, measured again when Change status opens). Right: **Pipeline** (3×2: Candidates, Shortlisted, Done
  appointment in alert, Offered, Hired in ledger, Not selected in danger;
  20px 600), **Actions** while open (pending: Owner + Note + Acknowledge;
  acknowledged: the owner in a peach row, *Helped by others* ticks open
  *Assisted by…*, one *Hired* pick per headcount, Hired through · Cost (RM)
  110px mono right-aligned, Note, **Mark filled**; always **Cancel request**
  on white), a closed card (*Filled · Siti Aisyah · via Jobstreet · RM 450* /
  *Cancelled*) with **Reopen**, and the **Audit trail** (All · Status only on a
  `240 238 234` switch): a status is a 10px dot (clay; the newest accent)
  with a STATUS tag, any other step a 7px ring; 1px rules between.
- **Windows** (scrim .22, radius 16, fade .2s): *Add candidate* / *Edit
  candidate* 560px (Name *, Phone, Status, the candidate fields, Reference /
  special note, a dashed résumé row, *Change which fields appear here*);
  *Candidate fields* 620px (Field · Type · Dropdown choices · ×, *+ Add
  field*, Done — saved on close); *Pick from Talent Bank* 560px (name 500,
  the position 12px, *Matches* in ledger, the first line of their notes, Add);
  *Move to Talent Bank* 560px (Name *, Phone, Email, Department *, Good fit
  for (position) *, Why keep them, the résumé).
- **The résumé** — the 600px drawer: `HR-203 · REVENUE SPECIALIST · DONE
  APPOINTMENT` (10px 600 .14em clay), the name 19px, the file mono, Download;
  the file on `236 233 228` (a PDF framed, an image as it is, a Word document
  on a white A4 sheet saying it opens in Word); the note at the foot.
- **Phone width**: the inbox, the page and the new-request grid fall to one
  column, the stats two to a row, the listing and Form settings scroll

### Hiring › Insights and the Talent Bank (v3.33, 2026-10-11)

The rest of **V. HIRING** (README §12–13; the handoff's `Revenue App.dc.html`
lines 1145–1270, the drawers 1334–1364 and 1484–1497). Values in
`hiring/_components/hiring.module.css`.

- **Insights** (`/hiring/insights`; how long each request takes, from
  submitted to acknowledged to filled — the page no longer says so, v3.75). The period bar: a white segmented
  box (3px, radius 10) — **This month** · **All time** · a 1px rule ·
  **Pick a month** (calendar and chevron; the picked one dark `43 31 23` on
  cream, 500) — then the range in words (`1 Oct 2026 – 31 Oct 2026`, 12.5px).
  Pick a month opens a 264px card (radius 12, 12px; on a phone leftwards from
  the button's right edge, so every month is on screen): *LAST 12 MONTHS*, then a
  4-column grid of months (`Oct` 13px 500 over `2026` 10px at .7). **Six
  cards** in a row (gap 9, 146px tall, the kit's card look): Requests
  submitted · People hired (a ledger bar) · Avg days to acknowledge · Avg days
  to fill · Hiring cost · Cost per hire (alert bars); the figure 28px, 22px
  past six characters; at the foot the change on the month before
  (`▲ 12% vs Sep` — ledger when it is good news, danger when not, `0% vs Sep`
  brown; none for all time) and the card's icon. **Days from submitted to
  filled**: the period's fills two to a row (`170px | bar | 44px`), the bar
  10px on `240 238 234` split into *To acknowledge* (`205 184 162`) and
  *Acknowledged to filled* (accent), longest first, with its legend. **By
  source** (people hired, `2 pax`, the cost) and **By department** (`1 open ·
  3 hired`, `25 days to fill` / *No fills*) side by side, each a 6px slate bar
  of its share. **Every request** scrolls sideways: 16 columns (`80px 230px
  140px 150px …`), the ID sticky, money and days mono and right-aligned, an open
  request's *N d so far* in alert. **Company** stands before Department (v3.67,
  the user, 2026-10-12): a guest's company, else *HOUZS Venture* — a guest's
  Department is its own company's, so the column says whose it is; *By
  department* still groups by the name alone. A row opens the **440px panel**: `HR-202 ·
  Revenue · Fair`, the title 19px, its chip and owner, a ledger *Hired: …*
  band, its facts (`150px | 1fr`; budget, cost and start in clay 600), the
  Audit trail, **Close** and **View full →**. A request is in the period when
  it was submitted or filled in it.
- **Talent Bank** (`/hiring/talent`; no subtitle since v3.75) **is a Drive since v3.74** (2026-10-12 — the
  user: "like google drive as well, can create new file, and name as well …
  so when save or upload can detect and locate file"), drawn with the Data
  center's own parts (*Data center as a Drive*, v3.69 — its bar, cards, ⋮
  menus and *New folder* window, imported, not copied): **+ New** (*New
  folder*, *Add talent* where people are kept, *Upload files*), the path
  (`Talent Bank › Carres › Sales`), *Search the Talent Bank* (places, people
  by name, what they fit, phone, email or notes, files — each with where it
  is). The places: the bank shows **Companies** — HOUZS Venture and the
  subsidiaries of Admin › Guest Link (one taken off that list stays while
  something is in it, its line ending *· off the guest link*) — then the
  **Folders** HR made there; a company its **Departments** (HOUZS Venture's
  are Hiring › Form settings' Department by first part, a subsidiary's its
  guest-link list) and its folders; a department or a folder its folders,
  then **People** (the Data center's person card: name, *what they fit ·
  Available* or *· Interviewing · HR-203*, a ⋮ with **Open**, **Move to…**,
  **Remove from Talent Bank** asked twice), then **Files** (the Data
  center's file cards; ⋮ View, Download, **Move to Bin** — once, since
  v3.77). A folder HR made has a ⋮ of **Rename**, **Move to…** (with
  everything in it) and **Move to Bin**; a department, while something is right in it, **Move everyone
  to…**; a company none. (The grey hint under the path that said where each place's
  folders come from went in v3.75.) Files may be dropped onto the page. **Move to…** is one
  440px window for the three — *Move Nur Iman* / *Move Seniors* / *Move
  everyone in Sales*, under it where it is now (*— everything in it goes
  with it*; *2 people, 1 folder — now in …*), a finder, every company,
  department and folder top down (indented 18px a level; for a folder the
  bank itself first, never itself or what is inside it; the place it is in
  *Here now* and greyed), **Move here**.
  **+ Add talent** is the 520px drawer (*Add to Talent Bank* · *Someone
  worth calling back when a role opens.*), opened in a place and keeping
  them there — *Kept in Carres › Sales* at its top instead of a Department
  field; name, phone, email, what they fit, their files, why keep them;
  *Save talent*. A person opens the **460px panel**: `T-103`, the name
  19px, the chip; their facts (*Good fit for*, **Kept in**, phone, email,
  saved from, saved), *HR NOTES*, their files, *LATEST*; **Remove** (asks
  once more: *Confirm remove*), **Move to…**, **Close**, **Full page →**.
  The place is the page's address (`?in=dept:hv:Revenue`). *(Until v3.74 a
  260px tree of departments and positions beside a table.)* On a request's
  page, *Move to Talent Bank* says **Kept in Talent Bank › Carres › Sales**
  above the files; a guest's candidate has no Department field (their
  company's department is the request's), HOUZS Venture's keeps it.
  **The Bin** (v3.77, 2026-10-13 — the user: "make a bin, then that bin
  will last for 30 days"): a **Bin** pill after the search (the search's
  40px `panel-3` pill, a 16px bin glyph, its count in 11px mono brown;
  hover `row-hover`) opens `?in=bin` — the path *Talent Bank › Bin*, no
  + New, no search, and on the right **Empty bin** (a white 40px pill,
  `danger` text on a `danger-border` rim, the armed `danger-bg` for
  *Confirm empty bin*). Its sections are a place's — **Folders**, **People**,
  **Files** — drawn with the same cards: a folder's and a person's line
  *HOUZS Venture › Revenue › Interns · 29 days left* (where it was, through
  a folder in the Bin too), a file's sub line where it was (a person's file:
  *IC · Joyce Tan*) and its foot *12 Oct 2026 · Grace Lim* … *29 days
  left*. Each ⋮: **Restore** (a turning-back arrow) and **Delete forever**
  (asked once more: *Confirm delete forever*); without Edit, no ⋮ and no
  Empty bin. A card pressed says so in the toast (*… is in the Bin —
  restore it to open it.*); empty, the dashed block *The Bin is empty.* ·
  *What is deleted in the Talent Bank waits here for 30 days, then goes for
  good.* Toasts: *… moved to the Bin*, *… restored to Carres › Sales*,
  *… deleted forever*, *Bin emptied · 4 items deleted forever*.
- **A talent's page** (`/hiring/talent/[id]`): *‹ Talent Bank*, `T-104`, the
  name 24px, *Good fit for … · saved 24 Sep 2026 by Grace Lim*, the chip;
  `1fr | 340px`. **Profile** tiles (`minmax(170px, 1fr)`, values 500), **HR
  notes** (a 14px / 1.7 textarea, saved as it is written), the résumé (a 38px
  tile, **Open**, **Replace**); on the right **Invite to interview** (the open
  requests, the one that fits picked first; or *No open hiring requests right
  now.*) and **Remove from Talent Bank** (asked twice), then **History**
  (8px dots, the newest in the accent).
- On a request's page a candidate who is in the bank offers **★ Open in
  Talent Bank** (ledger).

### Hiring files (v3.46, 2026-10-11)

The user's answers on round 2: a candidate and a Talent Bank person keep
**several files, each with a file type** (mig `20261011160000`). No prototype
draws it: a file is the résumé row it replaces. Values in
`hiring/_components/hiring.module.css` (`.hf*`, `.cvPill`); the parts are
`HiringFiles.tsx`.

- **A file** is a row in a hairline box (radius 10, 12px; rows 8px apart):
  the 36px olive extension tile (`PDF`, `JPG`), the name 500 (cut with an
  ellipsis), its type 12px brown under it, then what may be done. Taken off in
  a window it is struck through in grey, *IC · removed on Save*, with **Undo**.
- **The type** — a 184px soft dropdown: *Choose type…*, Résumé, IC,
  Certificate and the types HR named, then (Hiring · Edit) **+ Add a new file
  type…**, which opens a name field (*e.g. Driving licence*) under it, its
  right edge with the picker's. A file chosen while the record has no résumé
  starts as Résumé; any other starts unpicked, and Save asks *Pick what kind of
  file it is.*
- **Add / Edit candidate** and **+ Add talent**: the files on record (Edit:
  Remove / Undo, applied on Save), then each chosen file (*Uploads on Save*,
  its type, ×), then the dashed box — *Upload résumé* while there is none,
  else *Add another file* · *Choose file* (several at once). Save sends the
  files up from the browser, then files the form.
- **The files drawer** (the 600px résumé drawer): the head names the file shown
  (`Résumé · Daniel_Lee_CV.pdf`, mono) and **Download** takes it; with more
  than one file, a row of pills under the head (12px, 4px 10px, radius 999,
  hairline; the one shown peach on an accent rim), the résumé first. The
  candidate's button reads *View résumé* for one résumé and *View files*
  otherwise; ⋯ *Download résumé* takes the newest résumé.
- **Move to Talent Bank** lists them all in its row (*Résumé · CV.pdf, IC ·
  ic.jpg*): they go with the candidate.
- **The Talent Bank panel**: a hairline row per file with **View**; *No files*
  beside a dash tile.
- **A talent's page**: the **Files** card — *FILES* and **+ Add file** (Create
  or Edit); rows on the card's own rules with a 38px tile, **Open**,
  **Replace** and **Remove** (asked twice, in the armed pink). *+ Add file*
  opens a row under a rule: the type, **Choose file** (filed as soon as it is
  chosen), Cancel. *No files yet.* when there are none.
- **Phone width**: a row wraps — its buttons, or the type and its ×, onto a
  second line; nothing runs past the screen.
- **Form settings › File types** (v3.48 — the user: rename and turn off): a
  third card after *Hiring sources*, drawn as it is — *File types*, its lede,
  cards `minmax(220px, 1fr)` (9px 12px, radius 8): the name 500 and `N files`
  mono 11px, a 22px **✎** (a quiet `panel-2` hover — renaming is no danger)
  that swaps the name for a field in its place (2px 8px, 13px; Enter or
  leaving it keeps the name, Esc lets it be), and the sources' **On / Off**
  switch; a type turned off is greyed (`quiet`). Résumé says *Built in* (11px
  brown) — no ✎, no switch. Then *New type, e.g. Driving licence* and
  **+ Add type**. A type turned off leaves the picker; the files filed under
  it keep it.

### Meeting Rooms (v3.34, 2026-10-11)

Admin › Meeting Rooms, `/admin/rooms` (README §14; the handoff's `Revenue
App.dc.html` lines 890–938, the drawers 1284–1332), in every view's sidebar —
HR's Admin section above Audit Log, the old shell's Reports & Admin. Values in
`admin/rooms/_components/rooms.module.css`.

- **Schedule** (the first tab; the tabs show only to whoever may manage the
  rooms). The toolbar: a date pager (‹ · `Sat, 10 Oct 2026 · today` 500 in a
  190px label · ›) in one `229 228 223` box radius 6 — the label names the
  asked day at once and nothing dims while its grid is read — **Today**, the day's
  count in words (12.5px `90 78 68`), then the legend (10px swatches radius 3,
  mono 11.5px 600 codes) and **+ Book a room** (the accent, black, 8px 18px).
  **The label opens a month** (v3.51, the user): it is a button — a 13px
  calendar mark in brown before the day, the `240 238 234` wash on hover and
  while open — and drops a 264px card under the pager (radius 12, 12px in, the
  Hiring month card's edge and shadow): ‹ `October 2026` (13px 600) › turn only
  the card's month; `MO … SU` (10px 600 brown, .06em); six weeks, Monday first,
  in 32px cells radius 7 (13px; the months either side `170 160 150`; today
  ringed 1.5px in the accent; the asked day dark `43 31 23` on cream). A day
  pressed goes there and closes the card — ‹ › on the pager still step one
  day; Escape, a press outside, or the day shown close it; the arrow keys move
  a day or a week.
  The grid: radius 10 in a `216 210 200` edge, at most 640px tall inside it
  (content-box, 642 in all) and scrolling; a 56px hour column (mono 10.5px) then one column per room
  (`minmax(170px, 1fr)`) under a sticky `235 231 224` head (name 500 13px,
  `8 seats · Level 3` 11px). **08:00–20:00, 52px an hour**, half-hour rules
  drawn by one repeating gradient. A meeting is a block 4px in from each side,
  1px down and 3px short, its department's light tone with a 3px deep-tone
  left edge: title 500 12px, the time mono 10.5px, `RV · Chea Huan · 6 pax`
  11px — hover rings it in the accent. **The free half hour under the pointer
  lights** (v3.51, the user — you see which bar the pointer is on): the kit's
  row hover `255 245 233`, the accent's 3px inset left edge where a meeting's
  own edge would be, and `+ 09:30` (mono 10.5px brown, 10px in) — never under a
  meeting, only on a device that points, only from today on and for whoever may
  book. Pressing it opens Book at that half hour, an hour long but never past
  20:00 (the lit 19:30 books 19:30–20:00; until v3.51 it opened 19:00). A day
  already past neither lights nor opens Book — the database refuses it.
- **Department tones** (`lib/rooms/model.ts` `departmentBadge`): Revenue RV
  slate, HR clay, Finance FIN gold — the handoff's — then MKT walnut, IT slate,
  DO, DIR, VO, and any other its first three letters in walnut. A guest-link
  booking is olive under its subsidiary's code. The legend lists HQ first.
- **Book a room** — the 520px drawer (eyebrow MEETING ROOM in slate, head and
  body 18px 22px): Meeting title; Room · Date, From · To, Booked by · From
  department in pairs (every select the kit's `Select`); Attending · internal
  as chips (the row kept when nobody is listed, so the search sits 16px under
  the label) with a type-a-name search and six suggestions; external guests as
  name / company rows with *+ Add an external guest*; **Total pax** in a grey
  box with a 38px − 1 + stepper that never goes under the named count (− greys
  there) and says `· too many` in red past the room's seats. The footer's red
  line says the first problem in words — a clash reads `Meeting Room 1 is
  taken 09:00–10:00 by “Fair weekly sync”.` A guest-link booking opens without
  the who-fields.
- **A booking** — the 440px drawer: kicker `ROOM · DEPARTMENT` in slate, the
  title 19px, the day and time mono; facts (Booked by, From department, Room,
  Reference `BK-301`; a guest one Company and Department and `· guest link`);
  ATTENDING · N with the booker marked and outside guests in `160 90 30`; the
  audit trail (8px dots, the last in the accent). Whoever booked it sees
  **Cancel meeting** (asks once more: *Confirm cancel*) and **Edit**; anyone
  else reads *Only Chea Huan can cancel this meeting.*
- **Booked for somebody else** (v3.45, the user 2026-10-11 — review L3): a
  booking Grace made with *Booked by* Chea Huan reads **Grace Lim (for Chea
  Huan)** wherever a booker is drawn — a block's last line (`RV · Grace Lim
  (for Chea Huan) · 6 pax`), the drawer's *Booked by*, the Revenue app's and
  the HR app's schedules, the guest page's window — and *Only Grace Lim or
  Chea Huan can cancel this meeting.* (`bookerName` / `whoMayCancel`,
  `lib/rooms/model.ts`).
- **Rooms** — cards in `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(260px, 1fr))`, radius 12,
  16px: the name edited in place (15px 500), × to remove (refused while it has
  bookings ahead), Seats (70px mono) · Location · Facilities saved as you
  leave the field, the bookings ahead and a **Bookable** switch (28×16, `.2s`;
  a room switched off leaves the schedule and its card fades to .6); a dashed
  **+ Add meeting room** card. Rooms start empty.

### Guest link — Request manpower (v3.37, 2026-10-11)

The guest page's third card (README §16, guest-phone-04, guest-desktop-03;
`Guest Booking.dc.html`'s step-by-step form), measured against the canvas's 3b
phone and 3c computer artboards. The fields are Hiring › Form settings' — but
**Department lists the picked company's own departments** (v3.67, the user,
2026-10-12): Admin › Guest Link › Subsidiaries & departments, as Book a meeting
room's, never Form settings' HQ choices. It is the soft dropdown whatever its
length (*Choose…*); a company with none listed gives a 46px box to type it in
(*e.g. Operations*, 60 characters); picking another company empties it.

- **Steps** — *About you* (Company, Your name) → *The role* → *Candidate
  requirements* → *Job description* → *Check and send*. Over them the step's
  name (12.5px 600) with *Step 1 of 5* in grey, and five 4px bars, orange up
  to the step. Each step is one white card (radius 16, 18px): its title 15px
  600 and a grey line under it, then the fields two to a row from 400px
  (`minmax(200px, 1fr)`). A label is 12.5px 500 `90 78 68`, a red *
  when required, a grey *· optional* when not.
- **Fields** — text 46px; a number as a 46px − n + stepper (16px 600);
  a range as two 46px number boxes with *to*; a choice of five or fewer as
  pills (40px, radius 999, the picked one dark), more as the soft dropdown;
  long text as a five-line box (14px / 1.55); the file as a dashed
  `250 247 243` box with a 40px olive tile (`+`, then the file's kind),
  *Upload a file / PDF, Word or a photo* and *Choose* (*Replace*).
- **Buttons** — *Back* (white, 52px) and the orange *Next* twice as wide;
  *Review* on the last step, *Send to HR* on Check and send, where each step
  is a block of label / value rows with its *Edit*. Missing fields in one red
  line: *Fill in Company, Your name.* Done: ✓ *Sent to HR*, the request's
  number (mono) and *Send another request* (dark).
- **Desktop** — the 340px Preview: *Your request / This is what HR will
  see*, *n of 15 filled*, the four groups, the budget as *RM 2500 – 3000*.
- A phone keeps the portal's 16px floor for what is typed (globals.css; iOS
  zooms on anything smaller) — the text box is held to the prototype's five
  14px lines all the same.
### The HR phone app (v3.38, 2026-10-11)

`/hr-app` (README §17, the handoff's `HR Mobile App.dc.html`, the canvas's
artboard 4a) — HR's view of the ONE app on a phone (v3.50: no install of its
own; the portal's HR pages are the computer's), not a portal page. *More* ends
with the view switch for an account that may switch views: a second white card,
one row per view (Principal, Finance — the portal; HR — this app; Revenue team —
the Revenue app), a 10px dot in the view's colour on a `246 242 236` tile, the
current row reading *You are here* at .6 with no chevron. Values in
`components/hr-app/hr-app.module.css`, the handoff's own rgb values; the app
runs on the browser's defaults (line-height normal, content-box) like the
prototype, on the warm ground `246 242 236`, at most 560px wide and centred.

- **Head** — 52px: a 40px walnut tile (Home) or a 36px clay back chevron, an
  11px clay kicker over a 24px 700 title, and a walnut 36px action (*+ New*,
  *+ Book*) on the right. Pages slide in (`mPush .22s`).
- **Home** — the walnut hero (radius 20, 18px): *Good afternoon* 13px at .75,
  the first name 20px 700, three stat tiles (22px 700 figures — *To review*,
  *Open roles*, *Letters missing*); *Waiting for you* (40px tags HIRE / WAIT /
  REPL / CLAIM, *See all*) and *Today's meetings* (mono 12px times, a 3px bar —
  slate for HQ, orange for the guest link) as white radius-18 cards.
- **Review** — the inbox: All · Hiring · Member services chips with counts;
  hiring requests and member requests by their own numbers (`HR-205`,
  `RQ-2610-004`); a member request opens its facts with **Accept** /
  **Approve** and **Reject**. A claim's **Approve** (v3.45, the user
  2026-10-11) opens a small sheet, *Approve claim*: one 12.5px line (*Add to
  payroll pays it with the salary. Request payment sends it to Finance to pay
  on its own.*) over two 48px pills side by side — **Request payment**
  (white) and **Add to payroll** (orange) — Member services' two routes.
- **Hiring** — Open · Pending · In progress · Filled · Closed; a request card
  (its id mono, the status chip, the position, `dept · requester`, three pills:
  candidates, at appointment or further, out); its page: the requirement tiles,
  candidates with **Change status ▸**, *+ Add candidate*, the trail, and a bar
  pinned above the tabs — Decline / Request more info / Acknowledge while
  pending, Cancel / Mark filled once acknowledged.
- **Rooms** — *By room* | *Timeline* (64px an hour) and the seven day chips,
  as in the Revenue app; HQ slate, the guest link orange.
- **More** — Insights (days to fill, cost, sources), the Talent Bank, the Data
  center (everybody, a red `!` while a compulsory letter is missing; a
  person's letters with **View**).
- **Sheets** — rise from the bottom (`mSheet .28s`) over a walnut scrim;
  fields 46px radius 12; every select the kit's soft dropdown with its soft
  field (radius 10, the chevron — the canvas's global `select` rule); the
  buttons 48px pills.
- **Tab bar** — white, pinned, five tabs (Home · Review · Hiring · Rooms ·
  More), the active one clay; it stands on `--safe-bottom`.
- Not in the handoff: *No meeting rooms yet.* (Rooms) and *No meetings
  today.* (Home) — its sample always has both.

### Product List — a page of the Revenue app (v3.13, 2026-10-08)

The Product List left the portal for the Revenue app: `/member/products`,
inside the app's own shell (`RevenueApp`, the `[data-revenue-app]` palette),
drawn to the Claude Design handoff *design_handoff_product_list* — its README
is the spec, `prototype/ProductList.dc.html` the source of truth (both kept in
`.design/revenue-app/`). The portal's light `.product-theme` workspace
(approved 2026-09-24) is gone with it; `/revenue/products` redirects.

- **Shell.** The app's desktop sidebar shows **Product List** (cube icon) under
  a group label — PRINCIPAL for the Principal, MANAGEMENT for another holder of
  `product_list`; nobody else sees the row. Active row: #FFF1E6, ink #BD4A19,
  650 (the README's — the prototype reads undefined variables there and shows a
  grey browser button). Above the profile card the **Module** switcher
  (Principal only) opens Finance / Principal / HR / Revenue team.
- **Page.** Eyebrow REVENUE / PRODUCTS with its dash, the Georgia title,
  `Principal only · Fair and IR` on the right; the six tabs on the header's
  rule (SKU Master · Modular · Maintenance · Combo Pricing · Costing gaps ·
  Costing Setting — *Add-on / KPI Items*, the seventh, became Rules › KPI items
  on 2026-10-08). The tab holding unsaved edits carries a
  dot; leaving it asks in a bar under the rail, and the app's own nav confirms.
- **Values.** Every colour is a `--rv-*` token and every size the prototype's
  (`_components/products.module.css`, primitives in `_components/ui.tsx`).
  Tailwind's reset is undone where it changes the prototype's boxes: elements
  the prototype draws content-box (a picker row's 46px is inside its padding, a
  module tile's 26px inside its border), an input's own 1px 2px padding, the
  browser's #757575 placeholder. `.design/revenue-app/tools/measure-pl.mjs`
  measures the same elements on the prototype and the live page and lists
  every difference; what remains is data (column widths), state, the
  sidebar row above and Modular's board below. Weight 650 renders as 650 here
  (Inter variable); the prototype file loads only Inter 400–700, so it shows 700.
- **Motion.** As the prototype: a right-hand panel of the app (notifications,
  Profile) shifts the page 432px in 320ms `cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)`; a
  floating window fades its scrim in 220ms and rises from
  `translateY(14px) scale(.98)` in 260ms on the same curve; a toggle's knob
  moves in 160ms; the new-combo window appears without motion. Reduced motion
  turns them off.
- **Tables.** Wide sheets scroll in their own 560px window with a sticky header
  and a frozen first column; the lists grow with the page. Columns can still be
  dragged and resized (`ColumnTable`, a cookie scoped to `/member/products`).
- **Modular's board — the one place it leaves the prototype (2026-10-08).**
  The prototype laid white model cards on the white page, a 1.5px #EBDDD2 edge
  apart, and a not-offered card on #FBF7F3 — ΔL* 2.6 off white, which nobody
  sees; the user found the board flat. Each category now sits on a sand tray
  (`--rv-sand`, radius 18, padding 16/16/18, its dark head inside, the brand
  rule in `--rv-line-strong` so it still reads there). An offered model is a
  white card, a 1px `--rv-line` edge and a soft lift (`0 1px 2px` + `0 4px
  12px` of ink at 8% / 5%), 6.4 L* above the tray; a not-offered one is an
  empty slot — transparent, 1.5px dashed `--rv-muted`, no lift, its *Not
  offered* pill outlined. Hover deepens the lift (a slot washes white
  instead), 160ms; the open card is the prototype's.

### Shared portal rules

These six rules are non-negotiable for any new or amended page. The CSS / Tailwind tokens that back them live in `src/app/globals.css` and `tailwind.config.ts` — already wired up.

1. **Page header.** Every top-level route page starts with this block (no exceptions, even small pages):
   ```tsx
   <div className="page-head">
     <div>
       <div className="page-eyebrow">
         <em>I.</em> SECTION · PAGE NAME
       </div>
       <h1 className="page-title">Page Title</h1>
       <p className="text-graphite text-sm mt-3">…description…</p>
     </div>
     {/* optional right-side action button */}
   </div>
   ```
   The roman numeral inside `<em>` mirrors the sidebar section it belongs to. Eyebrow text is `UPPERCASE · UPPERCASE`, mono.

2. **Money cells use `.num`.** Never hand-roll `font-mono tabular text-ink`. Use `<td className="num">{formatMoney(x)}</td>`. Negatives get `<span className="neg">`. Headline figures use `.statement-amount` (or `.num-hero`) for Inter medium tabular digits; add `.compact` for narrow summary columns. Reserve `--num-ink` for the digit and `--graphite` for supporting units.

3. **Data tables use `.ledger-table`.** `<table className="ledger-table">` with `<th>` / `<td>` only — the class wires up surface bg, zebra rows, accent-bg hover, hairline borders, header padding, right-aligned `.num` cells. Stop writing `<table className="w-full text-sm"><thead><tr className="border-b…">…`.

4. **Section headings use `.sect-head`.** `<div className="sect-head"><h2>…</h2><span className="rule" /><span className="count">N ITEMS</span></div>`. Medium-weight upright sans title + hairline rule extending right + uppercase mono counter.

5. **Colors come from CSS variables, never hex.** Use Tailwind classes (`text-ink`, `text-num-ink`, `bg-paper`, `border-hairline`, `text-accent`, `text-danger`) or `rgb(var(--xxx))` inside SVG. Hardcoded `#xxxxxx` is forbidden outside `globals.css` itself.

6. **A table with five or more columns must fold for mobile.** Below `md` a
   phone has ~343px of content width — enough for two columns and a chevron.
   Mark the demoted columns `m-hide` + `data-label`, wrap the row in
   `<LedgerRow>`, and end the `<thead>` with `<th className="lt-toggle" aria-hidden />`.
   `SortableTable` does it from its column config instead. **Never fold a row
   that a single form wraps** — the panel would duplicate its named inputs and
   the stale hidden copy is what gets submitted; use `.table-scroll` there.
   Full rules, including the four cases that legitimately keep every column,
   are in **Responsive** below. A headline figure uses `.statement-amount`, which scales from 26px to 23px
   and 21px at smaller widths. Narrow summary columns use `.statement-amount.compact` (18px).

### Canonical reference pages

When you're not sure how a pattern looks in code, copy from one of these — they're the v3.0 reference implementation:

| Pattern | Reference file |
|---|---|
| Page header + hero number + ledger table | `src/app/(portal)/fund-out/page.tsx` |
| Multi-column hero summary | `src/app/(portal)/payments/page.tsx` |
| Hero number + side stats | `src/app/(portal)/director-salary/page.tsx` |
| Section-grouped tables | `src/app/(portal)/statements/page.tsx`, `src/app/(portal)/overdue/page.tsx` |
| Tab rail on the header's rule (`.page-head.tabbed` + `SlidingTabs`) | `src/app/(portal)/revenue/_components/config/CommissionSection.tsx`, `src/app/(portal)/revenue/[sub]/hierarchy/_components/HierarchyTabs.tsx` |
| **Sheet page: summary cards + ledger table with `tfoot` + status chips** | `src/app/(portal)/hr-costing/page.tsx` |
| Sheet page with a working grid under its cards | `src/app/(portal)/hr-costing/payroll/[period]/page.tsx` |
| The design source of the sheet (cards, rail, framed tables, person header) | `src/app/(portal)/revenue/[sub]/[period]/_components/CommissionWorkbench.tsx`, `CommissionMonthSummary.tsx` |
| Pure read-only ledger | `src/app/(portal)/audit-log/page.tsx` |
| Monthly payment statement + Salary / Statutory tabs + department tables | `src/app/(portal)/hr-costing/payment/page.tsx`, `_components/Overview.tsx`, `_components/DeptRows.tsx` |
| Sidebar (roman numerals + dot markers) | `src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx` |
| TopBar (greet + role badge) | `src/components/layout/TopBar.tsx` |
| Mobile nav (bottom tabs + sheet) | `src/components/layout/BottomTabBar.tsx`, `MoreSheet.tsx` |
| Folding a hand-rolled table for mobile | `src/app/(portal)/venture-pool/page.tsx` |
| Folding a config-driven table | `src/components/SortableTable.tsx` |

### Pages still on the legacy v2.0 chrome (clean up when you touch them)

`(portal)/fund-out/[id]`, `/fund-in/[id]`, `/fund-in/new`, `/fund-out/new`, `/payments/[id]`, `/payments/new`, `/payments/dept-requests`, `/payments/payroll-requests`, `/statements/[id]`, `/statements/[id]/print`, `/approvals/[id]`, `/approvals/payroll-run/[id]`, `/approvals/payroll-run/[id]/director-report`, `/billing/contracts/new`, `/billing/contracts/[id]/edit`, `/billing/requests`, `/dept-pool/[slug]`, `/dept-pool/marketing`, `/dept-pool/submissions`, `/dept-pool/contracts/new`, `/director-salary/[directorId]`, `/director-salary/[directorId]/print`, `/hr-costing/staff/*`, `/hr-costing/payroll/*`, `/reports/pnl`, `/reports/fund-summary`, `/reports/base-rate`, `/reports/claims`, `/opex`, `/operating-fund`, plus the HR + Dept Manager branches inside `/dashboard`. Each one inherited the deeper palette automatically but still needs `page-head` / `.num` / `.ledger-table` swap when it next gets edited.

### What to do for "add a new feature" or "amend a page"

1. Open the most similar reference page above and copy the structural pattern.
2. Use existing Server Actions / RPCs unchanged — backend is locked, this doc is **frontend only**.
3. If your page genuinely needs a new component (e.g. a new chart, new modal layout), add it under `## Component Vocabulary` in this doc *before* writing the code.
4. Run `npm run build` before committing — it catches type errors and missing imports.

## 设计定位

HOUZS Venture Portal (VP) 是 HOUZS Venture Sdn Bhd 的内部银行系统。Users = Principal + Finance + HR + Dept Manager。Real money, real decisions, real daily use.

**Visual thesis (2.0):**

> **VP is a warm research journal for a family office.** Friendly but precise. The Principal opens it every morning and feels like reading his own handwritten notes, not signing a notarized contract. Same paper aesthetic as old-school banking, less severity. Anthropic-flavored, not Bloomberg terminal.

**Mood:** Institutional calm with warmth. Plaster paper, Walnut surfaces and Harbour blue interactions. Georgia page titles, medium-weight Inter section headings and quiet, aligned figures. The Payment statement is the shared typographic reference.

---

## 不要做的

**AI slop blacklist (hard rejections):**
- Purple/violet/indigo gradients
- 3-column feature grid with icons in colored circles
- Centered everything
- Uniform `rounded-xl` on all elements
- Decorative blobs, floating circles, wavy SVG dividers
- Emoji as design elements (no 🟢🟡🔴⚠️ — use typeset italic + accent color)
- Colored left-border on cards
- Generic "Welcome to VP" / "Unlock your treasury" copy
- Drop shadows on cards (use hairline borders)
- Slate/cool-gray backgrounds (we use warm cream)
- Pure white (`#FFFFFF`) backgrounds
- Inter as display font (Inter is body-only in VP)

**Not doing:**
- ~~Dark mode in Phase 1~~ — Shipped. Warm charcoal palette, not cold inversion
- Chart-first dashboard — type-led, number-first
- Multi-color status rainbows — single rust accent + muted gold + muted green

---

## Typography

> **2026-09-11 — shared Payment statement typography.** The user approved the
> Payment page's numbers, font weights and hierarchy for the rest of the portal.
> Apply this typography within the existing Plaster / Walnut / Harbour palette;
> do not introduce route-specific chrome or a new color theme.

### Font stack

Inter, Chivo Mono and Noto Sans SC are loaded with `next/font` in `layout.tsx`.
Georgia is a system font and needs no download. Every display chain includes
`--font-cjk` for consistent Simplified Chinese fallback.

```css
:root {
  --font-display: Georgia, 'Times New Roman', var(--font-cjk), serif;
  /* --font-sans, --font-mono and --font-cjk come from next/font. */
}
```

Tailwind's legacy `font-serif` continues to alias Inter. Do not change that alias:
existing navigation, controls and labels use it. Actual display titles use
`.page-title` / `h1` or `font-display`. Only page titles use Georgia; sections,
buttons, navigation, labels and figures remain upright sans or mono.

### Roles and scale

| Role | Font | Size / weight | Usage |
|---|---|---|---|
| Page title | Georgia | 36px / 400; 30px below 680px | `.page-title`, visible `h1` |
| Section title | Inter | 18px / 500 | `.sect-head h2`, legacy `h2.font-serif` |
| Subsection title | Inter | 15px / 500 | Legacy `h3.font-serif` |
| Headline amount or count | Inter, tabular digits | 26px / 500; 23px below 1000px; 21px below 680px | `.statement-amount`, `.num-hero`; Payment's `.payment-amount` |
| Compact summary amount | Inter, tabular digits | 18px / 500 | `.statement-amount.compact` in narrow multi-column summaries |
| Dense ledger figures | Chivo Mono, tabular digits | 13.5px / 500 | `.num`; preserve decimal and column alignment |
| Body and navigation | Inter | 13px / 400; active items 500 | Body, `text-sm`, `text-base`; deeper nav labels may be 12.5px |
| Supporting copy | Inter | 12px / 400 | Page descriptions, explanatory text |
| Table header | Inter | 11px / 400 | `.ledger-table thead th`, sentence case |
| Page eyebrow | Chivo Mono | 11px / 500 | `.page-eyebrow`, restrained tracking |
| Dates, IDs, rates and metadata | Chivo Mono | 11–13px / 400–500 | `.tabular`, `.mono`, `font-mono` |
| Buttons | Inter | 12px / 500 | Existing padding and touch targets stay intact |
| CJK | Noto Sans SC | Inherits role | Consistent fallback after the Latin font |

Use medium emphasis for ordinary labels and totals. Tailwind `font-medium` and
`font-semibold` resolve to 500; `font-bold` resolves to 600 for stronger emphasis.
Avoid heavy 700-weight monetary figures and oversized 40–64px dashboard totals.
The legacy size utilities remain available for nonfinancial displays; new KPI
markup must use the semantic amount classes instead of size/weight utility stacks.

Do not replace `.tabular` globally with sans: it also represents dates and
reference codes. A headline value opts into `.statement-amount`; table values
remain `.num`. The amount class does not change color, financial calculations or
status semantics. Preserve existing group bands, zebra rows and responsive table
behavior. Payroll retains its compact row geometry and frozen columns.

Mobile form inputs retain their 16px type and 44px touch-target safeguards.

---

## Color Palette — Walnut (v3.1, 2026-08-31)

> **v3.1 replaced the cream / deep-green / rust palette.** The v3.0 values below the
> Walnut block are kept for reference only — they describe what shipped between
> 2026-04 and 2026-08 and are **no longer live**. Read this section first.

### The three values

| | RGB | Job |
|---|---|---|
| **Plaster** | `242 239 234` | The page. Warm neutral greige — warmth without cream's yellow cast. |
| **Walnut** | `90 62 43` | The **one** dark thing: hero cards, primary buttons, money ink, modal scrim. |
| **Harbour** | `46 92 138` | The **only** signal: active nav, links, focus rings. |

### Rule 1 — a surface is free, a signal is not

`--accent` must stay clear of the red / amber / green lanes, because those three are
load-bearing: `--danger` = overdue, `--alert` = pending, `--ledger` = settled. Users
learn them, so they can't move.

The v3.0 rust accent sat **4° from `--danger`** on the colour wheel (16.7° vs 12.7°),
with `--alert` a further 21° away — three of five meaningful colours crowded into one
25° wedge. An active nav item, an overdue badge and a pending warning were the same
broad hue separated only by lightness, which is why the accent never read as *"you are
here"* rather than *"something's wrong"*. **Minimum gap is now 40°.**

That leaves blue for the accent — and it's why the palette can still be warm. A
*surface* reports no status, so its hue is unconstrained: Walnut owns the hero card,
the buttons, the money ink and the scrim without any of this applying to it.

**Never propose a warm accent.** It is the one colour on the page that has to dodge.

### Rule 2 — dark is limited by area, not hue

Three coloured sidebar rails were tried and rejected (deep green, Prussian, basalt).
The variable that mattered was **area**: a full-height rail is the largest surface in
the app and the only one carrying no numbers, so any weight it holds is weight the
reader carries all day.

So the rail carries **no colour at all** — Plaster one step down (`232 227 219`),
floated as a rounded inset panel so the page colour runs behind it, separated by a
hairline rather than by weight. The dark moved to `.hero-walnut`, a card-sized object
holding the figure the page exists to show.

Corollary worth remembering: a *desaturated* dark never registers as a hue — the eye
files it as black. The fix for "this reads as black" is to **re-saturate**, not to
lighten.

### `.hero-walnut`

The one dark object. Applied to the `lg:col-span-7` hero column on Dashboard, Fund IN,
Fund OUT, Venture Pool and Dept Pool.

It re-declares the palette tokens for its subtree — the same technique as
`.sidebar-contrast` — so markup inside keeps using `text-num-ink` / `text-muted` /
`text-accent` unchanged and inverts for free, including SVG strokes bound to
`rgb(var(--ink))`. **Semantics are re-lit too** (`--danger`, `--ledger`, `--alert`),
because a hero carrying `text-danger` for a negative balance would otherwise render
dark red on dark walnut and disappear.

### Chrome — two floating panels

The sidebar rail and the header are **peers**: same tone (`--chrome`, Plaster one step
down), same 10px inset, same `rounded-panel` corners. Together they are the chrome;
the page runs behind and between them.

`--chrome` has its own token rather than borrowing `--surface-2` (zebra rows, table
headers) so the two can be tuned independently, and `.sidebar-contrast` reads
`var(--chrome)` rather than repeating the literal, so rail and header cannot drift.

**Tune the chrome step by measurement, not by eye.** The first attempt at
`232 227 219` was only **ΔL\* 4.1** from the page — inside the barely-perceptible
band. It looked fine in a mockup and read as *no header at all* on a 60px strip in
the real app. The shipped value `222 215 202` is **ΔL\* 8.3**. Rule of thumb:
ΔL\* ~4 is invisible, ~6 is noticeable, ~8–10 is unambiguously a separate surface.
Keep any future chrome above ~6, and re-tune `.sidebar-contrast`'s `--surface-2`
(nav hover) and `--hairline` alongside it — they sit relative to `--chrome`, and
if it moves without them the hover state silently disappears.

**Why not a bottom border on the header.** The v3.0 rule stopped dead where the
sidebar edge began, leaving an L-shaped band that never closed — the original
complaint this palette answers. Removing it fixed the corner but overshot: on
`bg-paper` the header had no definition at all and dissolved into the page. Giving it
the rail's surface resolves both, because nothing has to butt up against anything.

---

### Legacy v3.0 base (superseded — reference only)

These values come from the v3.0 readability pass. **Always reference via Tailwind classes (`bg-paper`, `text-ink`, `border-hairline`) — never copy the hex into a component.** The hex literals here exist only for designer reference; the runtime uses RGB triplets in `globals.css` so dark mode + `<alpha-value>` work.

```css
:root {
  --paper:      #F3ECDD;  /* warm cream — page background. Slightly more saturated than v2.0 to make ink/numbers stand out. */
  --surface:    #FAF5EA;  /* lifted paper — card background */
  --surface-2:  #E8DDC4;  /* subtle alt row for striped tables, table headers */
  --hairline:   #C6B99E;  /* warm pencil line — borders and dividers */
  --ink:        #1C160F;  /* dark brown-black — primary body text */
  --graphite:   #5A4E40;  /* warm muted text — secondary */
  --muted:      #85775F;  /* tertiary text — placeholder, disabled */
  --num-ink:    #1A130B;  /* NEW v3.0 — deepest tone, RESERVED for monetary digits + tabular data. */
}
```

> **Why `--num-ink` exists:** Finance users complained that money digits looked "dreamy" in v2.0. By giving digits their own one-tier-deeper color and reserving `--graphite` for the `RM` prefix, cents, and units, the digit jumps off the page. `--ink` stays for body and titles so reading is still calm.

**Token usage map:**

| Token | Use for | Don't use for |
|---|---|---|
| `--paper` | Page bg, sidebar bg | Cards (use `--surface`) |
| `--surface` | Card / panel bg | Page bg |
| `--surface-2` | Zebra rows in tables, table headers, hover bg | Card bg |
| `--hairline` | All borders, dividers, hr | Text |
| `--ink` | Body text, titles, default text | Money digits |
| `--num-ink` | **Money digits only** + hero figures | Body text |
| `--graphite` | Secondary text, RM/cents/units, table cell metadata | Money digits |
| `--muted` | Placeholder, disabled, "—" sentinels | Body text |

### Accent — single rust seal

```css
:root {
  --accent:     #CC5A2E;  /* rust / terracotta — THE accent */
  --accent-bg:  #FBE8DE;  /* rust light tint for hover, active nav, selected */
  --accent-border: #E8B098; /* rust border on focus states */
}
```

**Usage rules for the accent:**
1. Italic serif text of key labels ("*Free pool*", "*Pending your hand*", "*Approve*")
2. Active sidebar nav item (italic serif + `--accent-bg` background)
3. Primary action buttons ("Approve" on approval cards, "Save" on forms)
4. Focus ring on form inputs
5. **Nothing else.** No decorative rust. No rust dividers. No rust icons.

### Semantic colors — muted, not neon

```css
:root {
  --ledger:       #3D6B45;  /* positive — loan fully repaid, statement paid */
  --ledger-bg:    #E7EFE6;
  --alert:        #A07020;  /* warning — late_1 overdue, maturing soon */
  --alert-bg:     #F5EBD3;
  --danger:       #A3371A;  /* rare — late_2 / escalation overdue, critical */
  --danger-bg:    #F8E1D8;
  --info:         #5A6B8A;  /* subtle — pending, draft */
  --info-bg:      #EBEEF3;
}
```

**Semantic rules:**
- **Default status is silent.** Active loans have no color.
- Alerts use italic text + semantic color, never filled pills
- `--danger` is reserved for escalation-stage overdue only. Don't burn it on small errors.
- Overdue label format: `<em class="text-alert">overdue 15d</em>` — italic graphite-warm, not a red pill

### Dark mode — Shipped

**Warm charcoal palette** — not cold inversion. Maintains the journal aesthetic in low light.

**Architecture:** CSS variables (RGB triplets) in `globals.css` → Tailwind references via `rgb(var(--xxx) / <alpha-value>)`. Theme toggle in TopBar. System preference respected by default, manual override via `data-theme` attribute on `<html>`.

**Dark palette:**
| Token | Light (RGB) | Dark (RGB) |
|-------|-------------|------------|
| paper | 243 236 221 | 26 23 20 |
| surface | 250 245 234 | 36 32 25 |
| surface-2 | 232 221 196 | 46 41 34 |
| hairline | 198 185 158 | 61 54 44 |
| ink | 28 22 15 | 232 224 212 |
| graphite | 90 78 64 | 168 155 140 |
| muted | 133 119 95 | 115 103 88 |
| **num-ink** | **26 19 11** | **245 240 232** |
| accent | 204 90 46 | 224 107 58 |
| accent-hover | 181 74 34 | 240 122 72 |

**FOUC prevention:** Inline `<script>` in `<head>` reads localStorage before paint.

**Known limitations:**
- Recharts components (PnLChart, FundPieChart, BaseRateTrendChart) use hardcoded hex colors — chart elements may have reduced contrast in dark mode
- Print pages (statements, director salary) stay light-only (intended for physical printing)

### Contrast verification (v3.0 Finance-readability deepening)

All combinations exceed WCAG AA. Numbers updated for the deeper palette:

**Light mode:**
- `--num-ink` (#1A130B) on `--paper` (#F3ECDD) = **~14.0:1** ✓ (digits jump out)
- `--ink` (#1C160F) on `--paper` = **~13.5:1** ✓
- `--graphite` (#5A4E40) on `--paper` = **~7.0:1** ✓ (was 5.4 in v2.0 — significant improvement)
- `--muted` (#85775F) on `--paper` = **~4.4:1** ✓ (was 3.2 — now passes AA for normal text)
- `--accent` (#CC5A2E) on `--paper` = **~4.7:1** ✓
- `--accent` (#CC5A2E) on `--accent-bg` (#FBE8DE) = **~4.7:1** ✓ (active sidebar item)

**Dark mode:**
- `--num-ink` (245 240 232) on `--paper` (26 23 20) = **~14.5:1** ✓
- `--ink` (E8E0D4) on `--paper` (1A1714) = **12.1:1** ✓
- `--graphite` (A89B8C) on `--paper` = **5.9:1** ✓
- `--accent` (E06B3A) on `--paper` = **4.9:1** ✓
